You’re right. That was contradictory.

What caused it:
- `chief_health_check.sh` treated any failed nested operational check as “Chief needs attention.”
- But the actual nested issue was the smart briefing being stale while its cron jobs are intentionally paused.
- Then it ended with “DJ action: none,” which makes the alert framing wrong.

I fixed it:

- If something actually needs you:  
  ``​`text
  ⚠️ Chief needs DJ attention
  ``​`
- If it’s internal / non-DJ-actionable:  
  ``​`text
  ℹ️ Chief Hermes follow-up logged
  DJ action: none.
  ``​`
- If smart briefing is stale **because the briefing cron jobs are paused**, that is now skipped as an issue:
  ``​`text
  Smart briefing freshness: skipped because smart briefing cron jobs are paused
  ``​`

Verified current output now says:

``​`text
✅ Chief OK
DJ action: none.
``​`

And the operational health check now ends with:

``​`text
Status: OK
``​`

I also updated the daily morning health summary prompt so it does **not** say “attention needed” unless there is actual DJ action required.

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Operational health check pattern\n\nFor monitoring several file freshness checks + script probes in Python:\n\n``​`python\nISSUES: list[str] = []\nLINES: list[str] = []\n\ndef check_file(path, max_age_h=None, max_size_kb=None, min_size=1):\n    p = Path(path)\n    if not p.exists():\n        ISSUES.append(f\"Missing file: {path}\")\n        LINES.append(f\"{path}: MISSING\")\n        return\n    age = (time.time() - p.stat

### user

```text
[DJ Mauch] i dont understand what your "schedule" notation means
```

### assistant

```text
Totally fair — that notation is Unix **cron syntax**. It’s compact but not human-friendly.

It has 5 fields:

``​`text
minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week
``​`

So:

``​`text
15 7 * * *
``​`

means:

- minute: `15`
- hour: `7`
- day-of-month: `*` = every day
- month: `*` = every month
- day-of-week: `*` = every weekday/weekend

In plain English: **every day at 7:15 AM UTC**.

For our current jobs:

- `15 7 * * *`  
  → every day at **7:15 AM UTC**

- `0 */3 * * *`  
  → every **3 hours**, on the hour  
  → 00:00, 03:00, 06:00, 09:00, etc. UTC

- `0 12 * * *`  
  → every day at **12:00 PM UTC**

- `0 9,10 * * *`  
  → every day at **9:00 AM and 10:00 AM UTC**  
  → this is used for the 5AM ET ToM job because Eastern Time shifts with daylight savings; the script itself only does work when the current time is actually 5AM ET.

- `55 12,20 * * *`  
  → every day at **12:55 PM and 8:55 PM UTC**

- `5 13,21 * * *`  
  → every day at **1:05 PM and 9:05 PM UTC**

`*` just means “every possible value” for that field.
```

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Operational health check pattern\n\nFor monitoring several file freshness checks + script probes in Python:\n\n``​`python\nISSUES: list[str] = []\nLINES: list[str] = []\n\ndef check_file(path, max_age_h=None, max_size_kb=None, min_size=1):\n    p = Path(path)\n    if not p.exists():\n        ISSUES.append(f\"Missing file: {path}\")\n        LINES.append(f\"{path}: MISSING\")\n        return\n    age = (time.time() - p.stat

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Operational health check pattern\n\nFor monitoring several file freshness checks + script probes in Python:\n\n``​`python\nISSUES: list[str] = []\nLINES: list[str] = []\n\ndef check_file(path, max_age_h=None, max_size_kb=None, min_size=1):\n    p = Path(path)\n    if not p.exists():\n        ISSUES.append(f\"Missing file: {path}\")\n        LINES.append(f\"{path}: MISSING\")\n        return\n    age = (time.time() - p.stat

-x \"$PYTHON_BIN\" ]]; then\n    24|  PYTHON_BIN=\"python3\"\n    25|fi\n    26|\n    27|out=\"$($PYTHON_BIN /opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py --date \"$today\" --apply)\"\n    28|printf '%s' \"$today\" > \"$last_run_file\"\n    29|chmod 600 \"$last_run_file\"\n    30|\n    31|tmp_json=\"$(mktemp)\"\n    32|printf '%s' \"$out\" > \"$tmp_json\"\n    33|$PYTHON_BIN - \"$tmp_json\" <<'PY'\n    34|import json, sys\n    35|from pathlib import Path\n    36|j=json.loads(Path(sys.argv[1]).read_text())\n    37|if j.get('status') == 'noop':\n    38|    print(f\"Daily ToM sync: no-op for {j.get('date')} — {j.get('reason')}\")\n    39|else:\n    40|    c=j.get('counts', {})\n    41|    bg=j.get('by_group', {})\n    42|    print(\n    43|        \"Daily ToM sync complete\\n\"\n    44|        f\"Date: {j.get('date')}\\n\"\n    45|        f\"Source: {j.get('latest_source_section')}\\n\"\n    46|        f\"Carried: {c.get('carried', 0)} tasks\\n\"\n    47|        f\"Slack added: {c.get('slack_added', 0)} tasks\\n\"\n    48|        f\"Returning from parking: {c.get('returning_from_parking', 0)}\\n\"\n    49|        f\"Completed skipped: {c.get('completed_seen', 0)}\\n\"\n    50|        f\"Groups: Professional {bg.get('Professional', 0)} / MENA {bg.get('Professional - MENA', 0)} / Others {bg.get('Professional - Others', 0)} / Personal {bg.get('Personal', 0)}\"\n    51|    )\n    52|PY\n    53|rm -f \"$tmp_json\"\n    54|", "total_lines": 53, "file_size": 1705, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

### assistant

```text
Assuming everything is healthy, you should get **2 normal daily updates** in the alerts channel:

1. **Daily ToM sync** — around **5:00 AM ET**
2. **Daily Chief morning health summary** — around **8:00 AM ET**

The **every-3-hours health check should send zero messages when healthy**.

The backup job is a slight exception:
- **Daily cloud-state backup** runs around **3:15 AM ET**
- It only messages if it actually pushes a backup change, or if there’s a problem.
- So on a “healthy but something changed” day, you may get **3 total**.
- On a “healthy and no backup changes” day, it should be **2 total**.

So the practical answer is: **2 per day normally, possibly 3 if the backup sync pushes changes.**
```

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Operational health check pattern\n\nFor monitoring several file freshness checks + script probes in Python:\n\n``​`python\nISSUES: list[str] = []\nLINES: list[str] = []\n\ndef check_file(path, max_age_h=None, max_size_kb=None, min_size=1):\n    p = Path(path)\n    if not p.exists():\n        ISSUES.append(f\"Missing file: {path}\")\n        LINES.append(f\"{path}: MISSING\")\n        return\n    age = (time.time() - p.stat

The briefing reads DJ's Daily ToM first and uses it as the priority lens for Slack + email review.\n\n## Safety rules\n\n1. Never send, reply, forward, invite, share, comment, or notify anyone.\n2. Never write Slack/email-derived items into the Daily ToM doc without DJ's explicit approval.\n3. Suggested ToM additions are approval-required suggestions only.\n4. Do not persist raw Slack/email contents to Hermes memory.\n5. Keep durable facts/preferences in Hermes memory; keep operational rolling state in files.\n\n## Current files\n\n- Daily ToM sync: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py`\n- Daily ToM context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py`\n- Email context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/email-tom-context.py`\n- Slack collector: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_collect.py`\n- Slack deterministic pre-filter: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_filter.py`\n- Briefing context builder: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_context.py`\n- Rolling state: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/open_topics.md`\n- Briefing policy: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/BRIEFING_POLICY.md`\n- Final brief archive directory: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/`\n- Google security policy: `/opt/data/google-accounts/SECURITY_POLICY.md`\n- Attack-vector checklist: `/opt/data/security/ATTACK_VECTOR_CHECKLIST.md`\n\n## Model-routing posture\n\n- Dumb/API work: script-only, no LLM.\n- Slack crawl collection: no-agent Python script.\n- ToM sync: no-agent Python script.\n- Email/ToM/Slack context extraction: deterministic scripts.\n- Final executive synthesis: heavier model (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6`) because it requires judgment.\n- Simple health summaries: lightweight model (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5`).\n\n## Briefing structure\n\nSix sections, always in this order:\n\n1. **Executive summary** — 3-5 bullets max. Most important first. Decisive, not neutral.\n2. **Needs DJ attention** — items requiring a decision, response, or action. Opinionated: tell DJ what to do.\n3. **Worth knowing / monitor** — brief. No action today. One paragraph or less per item.\n4. **Deliberately excluded** — one-liner list showing what the filter caught and why. Builds trust.\n5. **Suggested ToM additions — approval required** — formatted ToM-style entries. Ask DJ directly for each.\n6. **Watchlist / carry-forward** — compact rolling state for next briefing.\n\nSee `references/slack-filter-calibration.md` for detailed content filtering rules.\n\n## Calibration rules (v3 — validated [REDACTED_PHONE])\n\n### The DJ relevance test\nEvery item must answer: **\"Why does DJ need to know this, and what decision or action follows?\"**\nIf the answer is weak → downgrade to \"Worth knowing\" or exclude. Do not fill the briefing with items that merely exist.\n\n### Hard exclusions (never include)\n- **Slackbot app install requests** — DJ is a workspace super admin and receives these automatically. They are IT's job. Hard-exclude at filter level, never surface in briefing.\n- **Pure bot test results / CI channels** — waves-dev-test-automation, test-android-results, backend-dev-testify-automation, application-status, plattest-012n3.\n- **Cleaning/housekeeping ops channels** — below DJ level.\n- **Platform degradation bot floods** — repeated bot-only degraded/outage lines with no escalation owner or recovery status.\n\n### Always include\n- **DMs and MPIMs** — always surface. But MUST read full context (all thread/prior messages) before interpreting. Never quote one line and guess meaning.\n- **Exact Daily ToM entities** — Easton, Wynwood, MENA, SICO, Olaya, Hubspot, F&B, Board deck, etc.\n- **Deal/acquisition/project channels** — proj-society-wynwood-acquisition, proj-mena, proj-easton, and similar.\n- **Money, legal, approval, deadline, investor, board signals** — wire, drawdown, capital, contract, signature, DD, due diligence, data room.\n\n### Include with judgment\n- **Tour/prospect signals in leasing channels** — people waiting for tours, prospect visits, staffing gaps at open houses. These ARE valuable to DJ even though \"leasing\" is otherwise a weak keyword.\n- **Engineering items with a named ToM entity** — product naming decisions (e.g., \"Society Wynwood → Flow Wynwood\"), blockers, or product decisions linked to active deals.\n- **Notable engineering discussions** — include when there is a real decision or entity; exclude low-context chatter (PR merges, minor code comments, identity creation, color fixes).\n\n### DM / group-DM handling\n- Always surface DMs/MPIMs.\n- Read **all context lines** provided before drawing any conclusion.\n- If a message is ambiguous without context (e.g., \"let's wind him down the week after next\"), report: what you know (participants, instruction given), what you don't know (subject), and provide the link. **Do not guess.**\n- If context is insufficient: \"Group DM with [names] mentions [instruction] — subject not identifiable from available context. Link: [url].\"\n\n### False inference rule\n- **Never connect unrelated items** as if they are related.\n- Example: A Vercel access approval for a MENA app does NOT mean MENA KPI/Hubspot/revenue diagnostic work is unblocked — they are completely separate. Only state connections that are **explicitly** supported by the source mat

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\nWhen reporting cron jobs to DJ, **do not show raw cron notation as the primary format.** DJ explicitly called that absurd/non-human. Translate schedules into plain English and ET-local next-run times, e.g.:\n\n- `Runs: every 3 hours, on the hour`\n- `Runs: every day at 5:00 AM ET`\n- `Next run: tomorrow at 8:00 AM ET`\n\nOnly include the raw cron string if DJ asks for implementation detail or you need it for debugging.\n\nWhen DJ a

{"success": true, "name": "daily-business-briefing", "description": "DJ's single ToM-aware Slack/email executive briefing workflow.", "tags": ["briefing", "slack", "gmail", "daily-tom", "chief-of-staff"], "related_skills": [], "content": "---\nname: daily-business-briefing\ndescription: \"DJ's single ToM-aware Slack/email executive briefing workflow.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: private\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [briefing, slack, gmail, daily-tom, chief-of-staff]\n---\n\n# Daily Business Briefing\n\nUse when configuring, debugging, or running DJ's single integrated smart business briefing.\n\n## Core principle\n\nThere is **one** business briefing, not separate Slack/email/ToM reports. The briefing reads DJ's Daily ToM first and uses it as the priority lens for Slack + email review.\n\n## Safety rules\n\n1. Never send, reply, forward, invite, share, comment, or notify anyone.\n2. Never write Slack/email-derived items into the Daily ToM doc without DJ's explicit approval.\n3. Suggested ToM additions are approval-required suggestions only.\n4. Do not persist raw Slack/email contents to Hermes memory.\n5. Keep durable facts/preferences in Hermes memory; keep operational rolling state in files.\n\n## Current files\n\n- Daily ToM sync: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py`\n- Daily ToM context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py`\n- Email context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/email-tom-context.py`\n- Slack collector: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_collect.py`\n- Slack deterministic pre-filter: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_filter.py`\n- Briefing context builder: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_context.py`\n- Rolling state: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/open_topics.md`\n- Briefing policy: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/BRIEFING_POLICY.md`\n- Final brief archive directory: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/`\n- Google security policy: `/opt/data/google-accounts/SECURITY_POLICY.md`\n- Attack-vector checklist: `/opt/data/security/ATTACK_VECTOR_CHECKLIST.md`\n\n## Model-routing posture\n\n- Dumb/API work: script-only, no LLM.\n- Slack crawl collection: no-agent Python script.\n- ToM sync: no-agent Python script.\n- Email/ToM/Slack context extraction: deterministic scripts.\n- Final executive synthesis: heavier model (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6`) because it requires judgment.\n- Simple health summaries: lightweight model (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5`).\n\n## Briefing structure\n\nSix sections, always in this order:\n\n1. **Executive summary** — 3-5 bullets max. Most important first. Decisive, not neutral.\n2. **Needs DJ attention** — items requiring a decision, response, or action. Opinionated: tell DJ what to do.\n3. **Worth knowing / monitor** — brief. No action today. One paragraph or less per item.\n4. **Deliberately excluded** — one-liner list showing what the filter caught and why. Builds trust.\n5. **Suggested ToM additions — approval required** — formatted ToM-style entries. Ask DJ directly for each.\n6. **Watchlist / carry-forward** — compact rolling state for next briefing.\n\nSee `references/slack-filter-calibration.md` for detailed content filtering rules.\n\n## Calibration rules (v3 — validated [REDACTED_PHONE])\n\n### The DJ relevance test\nEvery item must answer: **\"Why does DJ need to know this, and what decision or action follows?\"**\nIf the answer is weak → downgrade to \"Worth knowing\" or exclude. Do not fill the briefing with items that merely exist.\n\n### Hard exclusions (never include)\n- **Slackbot app install requests** — DJ is a workspace super admin and receives these automatically. They are IT's job. Hard-exclude at filter level, never surface in briefing.\n- **Pure bot test results / CI channels** — waves-dev-test-automation, test-android-results, backend-dev-testify-automation, application-status, plattest-012n3.\n- **Cleaning/housekeeping ops channels** — below DJ level.\n- **Platform degradation bot floods** — repeated bot-only degraded/outage lines with no escalation owner or recovery status.\n\n### Always include\n- **DMs and MPIMs** — always surface. But MUST read full context (all thread/prior messages) before interpreting. Never quote one line and guess meaning.\n- **Exact Daily ToM entities** — Easton, Wynwood, MENA, SICO, Olaya, Hubspot, F&B, Board deck, etc.\n- **Deal/acquisition/project channels** — proj-society-wynwood-acquisition, proj-mena, proj-easton, and similar.\n- **Money, legal, approval, deadline, investor, board signals** — wire, drawdown, capital, contract, signature, DD, due diligence, data room.\n\n### Include with judgment\n- **Tour/prospect signals in leasing channels** — people waiting for tours, prospect visits, staffing gaps at open houses. These ARE valuable to DJ even though \"leasing\" is otherwise a weak keyword.\n- **Engineering items with a named ToM entity** — product naming decisions (e.g., \"Society Wynwood → Flow Wynwood\"), blockers, or product decisions linked to active deals.\n- **Notable engineering discussions** — include when there is a real decision or entity; exclude low-context chatter (PR merges, minor code comments, identity creation, color fixes).\n\n### DM / group-DM handling\n- Always surface DMs/MPIMs.\n- Read **all context lines** provided before drawing any conclusion.\n- If a message is ambiguous without context (e.g., \"let's wind him down the week after next\"), report: what you know (participants, instruction given), what you don't know (subject), and provide the link. **Do not guess.**\n- If context is insufficient: \"Group DM with [names] mentions [instruction] — subject not identifiable from available context. Link: [url].\"\n\n### False inference rule\n- **Never connect unrelated items** as if they are related.\n- Example: A Vercel access approval for a MENA app does NOT mean MENA KPI/Hubspot/revenue diagnostic work is unblocked — they are completely separate. Only state connections that are **explicitly** supported by the source mat

### assistant

```text
That alert was from the old/bad health-check behavior.

What it meant:
- The smart briefing files were stale because the smart briefing had been paused for ~8 days.
- That was **not** a Chief/Railway failure.
- It also was not a DJ-actionable issue.
- So the alert wording was wrong: it should not have said **“Chief needs attention”** with **“DJ action: none.”**

I fixed the health check logic now:

- If smart briefing jobs are **paused**, stale smart briefing files are expected → no alert.
- If smart briefing jobs were just **turned back on for a trial** and the first scheduled run hasn’t happened yet, stale old files are expected → no alert.
- Only after the enabled briefing has had a real chance to run will stale briefing files count as a health issue.

I verified current health output now says:

``​`text
✅ Chief OK
DJ action: none.
``​`

So: **we’re treating that alert as obsolete/no-action**, and the check is corrected so it doesn’t complain before tomorrow’s smart briefing trial has actually run.
```

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\nWhen reporting cron jobs to DJ, **do not show raw cron notation as the primary format.** DJ explicitly called that absurd/non-human. Translate schedules into plain English and ET-local next-run times, e.g.:\n\n- `Runs: every 3 hours, on the hour`\n- `Runs: every day at 5:00 AM ET`\n- `Next run: tomorrow at 8:00 AM ET`\n\nOnly include the raw cron string if DJ asks for implementation detail or you need it for debugging.\n\nWhen DJ a

[truncated 3945 chars]

The briefing reads DJ's Daily ToM first and uses it as the priority lens for Slack + email review.\n\n## Safety rules\n\n1. Never send, reply, forward, invite, share, comment, or notify anyone.\n2. Never write Slack/email-derived items into the Daily ToM doc without DJ's explicit approval.\n3. Suggested ToM additions are approval-required suggestions only.\n4. Do not persist raw Slack/email contents to Hermes memory.\n5. Keep durable facts/preferences in Hermes memory; keep operational rolling state in files.\n\n## Current files\n\n- Daily ToM sync: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py`\n- Daily ToM context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py`\n- Email context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/email-tom-context.py`\n- Slack collector: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_collect.py`\n- Slack deterministic pre-filter: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_filter.py`\n- Briefing context builder: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_context.py`\n- Rolling state: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/open_topics.md`\n- Briefing policy: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/BRIEFING_POLICY.md`\n- Final brief archive directory: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/`\n- Google security policy: `/opt/data/google-accounts/SECURITY_POLICY.md`\n- Attack-vector checklist: `/opt/data/security/ATTACK_VECTOR_CHECKLIST.md`\n\n## Model-routing posture\n\n- Dumb/API work: script-only, no LLM.\n- Slack crawl collection: no-agent Python script.\n- ToM sync: no-agent Python script.\n- Email/ToM/Slack context extraction: deterministic scripts.\n- Final executive synthesis: heavier model (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6`) because it requires judgment.\n- Simple health summaries: lightweight model (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5`).\n\n## Briefing structure\n\nSix sections, always in this order:\n\n1. **Executive summary** — 3-5 bullets max. Most important first. Decisive, not neutral.\n2. **Needs DJ attention** — items requiring a decision, response, or action. Opinionated: tell DJ what to do.\n3. **Worth knowing / monitor** — brief. No action today. One paragraph or less per item.\n4. **Deliberately excluded** — one-liner list showing what the filter caught and why. Builds trust.\n5. **Suggested ToM additions — approval required** — formatted ToM-style entries. Ask DJ directly for each.\n6. **Watchlist / carry-forward** — compact rolling state for next briefing.\n\nSee `references/slack-filter-calibration.md` for detailed content filtering rules.\n\n## Calibration rules (v3 — validated [REDACTED_PHONE])\n\n### The DJ relevance test\nEvery item must answer: **\"Why does DJ need to know this, and what decision or action follows?\"**\nIf the answer is weak → downgrade to \"Worth knowing\" or exclude. Do not fill the briefing with items that merely exist.\n\n### Feedback ingestion rule\nWhen DJ returns a reviewed briefing with inline comments, treat each comment as calibration data and update the filter/prompt before generating another version. Convert corrections into explicit rules, not just one-off fixes. Common examples from prior calibration: Slackbot app-install requests are irrelevant; DM snippets require surrounding context; tour feedback/leasing wait-time signals can be useful; low-context engineering chatter should be excluded but notable engineering/product decisions should be included.\n\n### Hard exclusions (never include)\n- **Slackbot app install requests** — DJ is a workspace super admin and receives these automatically. They are IT's job. Hard-exclude at filter level, never surface in briefing.\n- **Pure bot test results / CI channels** — waves-dev-test-automation, test-android-results, backend-dev-testify-automation, application-status, plattest-012n3.\n- **Cleaning/housekeeping ops channels** — below DJ level.\n- **Platform degradation bot floods** — repeated bot-only degraded/outage lines with no escalation owner or recovery status.\n\n### Always include\n- **DMs and MPIMs** — always surface. But MUST read full context (all thread/prior messages) before interpreting. Never quote one line and guess meaning.\n- **Exact Daily ToM entities** — Easton, Wynwood, MENA, SICO, Olaya, Hubspot, F&B, Board deck, etc.\n- **Deal/acquisition/project channels** — proj-society-wynwood-acquisition, proj-mena, proj-easton, and similar.\n- **Money, legal, approval, deadline, investor, board signals** — wire, drawdown, capital, contract, signature, DD, due diligence, data room.\n\n### Include with judgment\n- **Tour/prospect signals in leasing channels** — people waiting for tours, prospect visits, staffing gaps at open houses. These ARE valuable to DJ even though \"leasing\" is otherwise a weak keyword.\n- **Engineering items with a named ToM entity** — product naming decisions (e.g., \"Society Wynwood → Flow Wynwood\"), blockers, or product decisions linked to active deals.\n- **Notable engineering discussions** — include when there is a real decision or entity; exclude low-context chatter (PR merges, minor code comments, identity creation, color fixes).\n\n### DM / group-DM handling\n- Always surface DMs/MPIMs.\n- Read **all context lines** provided before drawing any conclusion.\n- If a message is ambiguous without context (e.g., \"let's wind him down the week after next\"), report: what you know (participant

{"code": "import os, json, asyncio, time\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta\nfrom hermes_tools import terminal\n# Use a Python script with slack_sdk if available, loading token from /opt/data/.env\nscript = r'''\nimport os, asyncio, re, json, time\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta\n\ndef load_env():\n    p=Path('/opt/data/.env')\n    if p.exists():\n        for raw in p.read_text(errors='ignore').splitlines():\n            if raw.strip().startswith('SLACK_USER_TOKEN=') and 'SLACK_USER_TOKEN' not in os.environ:\n                os.environ['SLACK_USER_TOKEN']=raw.split('=',1)[1].strip().strip('\"').strip(\"'\")\n\ndef clean(t): return re.sub(r'\\s+',' ',t or '').strip()[:300]\ndef fmt(ts): return datetime.fromtimestamp(float(ts), timezone.utc).strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M UTC')\nasync def main():\n    load_env()\n    token=REDACTED'SLACK_USER_TOKEN')\n    if not token:REDACTED        print('NO_TOKEN'); return\n    from slack_sdk.web.async_client import AsyncWebClient\n    client=AsyncWebClient(token=REDACTED    auth=await client.auth_test(); dj=auth.get('user_id')\n    # find users matching sanya/levi\n    users=[]; cursor=None\n    while True:\n        resp=await client.users_list(limit=200, cursor=cursor)\n        users.extend(resp.get('members') or [])\n        cursor=(resp.get('response_metadata') or {}).get('next_cursor')\n        if not cursor: break\n    targets=[]\n    for u in users:\n        prof=u.get('profile') or {}\n        blob=' '.join(str(x or '') for x in [u.get('name'), u.get('real_name'), prof.get('display_name'), prof.get('real_name'), prof.get('email')]).lower()\n        if 'sanya' in blob or 'levi' in blob:\n            targets.append((u.get('id'), u.get('name'), u.get('real_name'), prof.get('display_name'), prof.get('email')))\n    print('TARGETS', json.dumps(targets, ensure_ascii=False))\n    # list ims map user->channel\n    ims=[]; cursor=None\n    while True:\n        resp=await client.conversations_list(types='im', limit=200, cursor=cursor)\n        ims.extend(resp.get('channels') or [])\n        cursor=(resp.get('response_metadata') or {}).get('next_cursor')\n        if not cursor: break\n    by_user={c.get('user'): c.get('id') for c in ims}\n    oldest=str(time.time()-14*86400)\n    for uid,name,real,disp,email in targets:\n        cid=by_user.get(uid)\n        print('\\nDM', uid, name, real, disp, 'channel', cid)\n        if not cid: continue\n        try:\n            hist=await client.conversations_history(channel=cid, oldest=oldest, limit=10)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print('ERR', type(e).__name__, str(e)[:100]); continue\n        msgs=sorted(hist.get('messages') or [], key=lambda m: float(m.get('ts',0)))\n        for m in msgs[-8:]:\n            sender='DJ' if m.get('user')==dj else (disp or real or name or m.get('user'))\n            print(f\"- [{fmt(m.get('ts'))}] {sender}: {clean(m.get('text'))}\")\nasyncio.run(main())\n'''\nres=terminal('python3 - <<\\'PY\\'\\n'+script+'\\nPY', timeout=180)\nprint(res['output'])"}

{"mode": "replace", "new_string": "def main():\n    if not LATEST.exists():\n        print('# Filtered Slack Business Brief Evidence\\\\nNo Slack collection file found.')\n        return\n    header, items = parse(LATEST.read_text(errors='ignore'))\n\n    high_signal = []\n    channel_pulse = []\n\n    for item in items:\n        value, tier, reasons = classify(item)\n        if value >= 105:\n            high_signal.append((value, tier, reasons, item))\n        elif value >= 20 and tier != 'Low confidence':\n            # Include lower-scoring items as business-pulse context so the model\n            # can synthesize what's happening across the workspace \u2014 not just DMs.\n            channel_pulse.append((value, tier, reasons, item))\n\n    high_signal.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)\n    channel_pulse.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)\n\n    # Dedup channel_pulse against high_signal by channel (keep at most 1 rep per channel)\n    seen_channels_pulse = {item['channel'] for _, _, _, item in high_signal}\n    deduped_pulse = []\n    for entry in channel_pulse:\n        ch = entry[3].get('channel', '')\n        if ch not in seen_channels_pulse:\n            seen_channels_pulse.add(ch)\n            deduped_pulse.append(entry)\n        if len(deduped_pulse) >= MAX_ITEMS_CHANNEL_PULSE:\n            break\n\n    ranked = high_signal[:MAX_ITEMS] + deduped_pulse\n    print(render(header, ranked))\n", "old_string": "def main():\n    if not LATEST.exists():\n        print('# Filtered Slack Business Brief Evidence\\\\nNo Slack collection file found.')\n        return\n    header, items = parse(LATEST.read_text(errors='ignore'))\n    ranked = []\n    for item in items:\n        value, tier, reasons = classify(item)\n        if value >= 105:\n            ranked.append((value, tier, reasons, item))\n    ranked.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)\n    print(render(header, ranked[:MAX_ITEMS]))\n", "path": "/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_filter.py"}

### assistant

```text
Got it. The Sanya/Levi framing was the wrong fix. I've reverted that and made two real changes instead:

**Problem 1 — DMs were being missed:** The broad workspace search was advancing the timestamp watermark, causing recent DMs to fall below the "already seen" cutoff. Fixed with a generic 48-hour DM+MPIM refresh pass that runs independent of the broad watermark.

**Problem 2 — Channel business activity was completely invisible:** The filter was cutting anything that scored below 105, which meant all workspace channel activity was dropped before the model saw it. Fixed by adding a second "business pulse" tier that passes channel items scoring above 20, deduplicated by channel, so the model can actually synthesize what happened across the business.

**Also fixed the briefing prompt** to require a non-empty *Business pulse / worth knowing* section covering channel activity — so if the filter does pass channel content, the model is instructed to synthesize it rather than ignore it.

The 5:05 PM ET briefing tonight will be the first run with these changes. If it's still missing things that should clearly be there, that's useful signal to calibrate further.
```

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\nWhen reporting cron jobs to DJ, **do not show raw cron notation as the primary format.** DJ explicitly called that absurd/non-human. Translate schedules into plain English and ET-local next-run times, e.g.:\n\n- `Runs: every 3 hours, on the hour`\n- `Runs: every day at 5:00 AM ET`\n- `Next run: tomorrow at 8:00 AM ET`\n\nOnly include the raw cron string if DJ asks for implementation detail or you need it for debugging.\n\nWhen DJ a

Most important first. Decisive, not neutral.\n2. **Needs DJ attention** — items requiring a decision, response, or action. Opinionated: tell DJ what to do.\n3. **Worth knowing / monitor** — brief. But MUST read full context (all thread/prior messages) before interpreting.
